# Web UI inventory: goodvibes-webui to products/webui

Current authorized source: [`dadf57700668fe4500b17b0b0b4520715ab25ef4`](https://github.com/mgd34msu/goodvibes-webui/tree/dadf57700668fe4500b17b0b0b4520715ab25ef4), WebUI 2.0.0, exactly **666 Git-tracked paths**. This is the actual published UI port target, not a version-only metadata bump. The original inventory was read against `9856cba64bb7df5677859c846a80eeb5c2da67c0` (1.13.19; 608 paths). The later recorded `505048329bae0076acff1f65b760e7a2dbd4609f` (1.13.20; 608 paths) was not the original read baseline. The complete provenance and removal ledger is [webui-upstream-delta.md](../audit/webui-upstream-delta.md).

The owner authorized integrating the published v2.0 UI and removing obsolete standalone/filler test and CI infrastructure. That cleanup extends the original inventory's restricted DROP rule; it does not authorize dropping route-backed product behavior, gate-preset replacements or contract-tree judgments. The 2026-09-26 ruling that keeps the permission sheet/lib as JEV still applies. The >80-character description/editorial planning-identifier gates are retired, with no new judgment battery required to replace them.

PORT carries the exact current upstream shell/components/styles and browser interactions, with actual workspace/import/build adapters where required. JEV preserves the remaining behavior while moving semantic readings, contract-tree facts and gate presets to the engine's authenticated boundary. HOIST would move a whole platform module into the engine; no new complete target module warrants it. A renderer can stay PORT while its helper remains JEV. Deterministic security boundaries, money arithmetic, input/URL/JSON grammar, machine ids and genuinely closed producer-written states remain code. Existing requirements to replace tolerant substring tests over closed states with exact values still apply. No old heuristic is a fallback for a judgment outage.

Each target path occurs once in the parseable three-column prefix consumed by `product-workspace-contract.ts`. Unchanged blobs retain their source-backed baseline rationale. Changed existing rows name the target delta first and retain the old rationale explicitly as historical context; old line numbers, removed selectors and former test assertions do not assert current source or require their restoration. New/renamed rows are assessed against dadf577. Dispositions describe required work, **not completed implementation**. Accounting parity does not prove semantic migration, generation, bundle safety, live daemon behavior, visual parity or passing tests.

Current implementation evidence and still-open acceptance dependencies are tracked separately in [webui-acceptance-reconciliation.md](../audit/webui-acceptance-reconciliation.md), at merged main `1941b66e` on 2026-10-05. Its verified browser runs and merged contract/session slices supersede historical missing-proof statements; the source dispositions below remain unchanged requirements, not completion claims. The [autonomous Jev decision contract](../design/autonomous-jev-decisions.md) governs new semantic consumers; historical escalation/owner-pick wording does not authorize a new human-approval workflow.

Files: 666. PORT 597, JEV 62, HOIST 0, DROP 7.

L84-89, L99, L148, L233 missing-file and JSON parse guards decide whether a source answered; choice: code (input shape guards). L124 `daemonSupportsWebuiCommand` `/\bwebui\b/.test(help)` decides whether the installed daemon binary offers a `webui` subcommand; now the word "webui" appearing anywhere in its free-text `--help` output; choice: Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.devserver.cli-subcommand` (the regex stands in for "does this help listing offer webui as a subcommand"; a description line elsewhere that mentions the webui satisfies it, and a false yes runs `webui status --json` on a build that boots a second daemon for unknown arguments). L156-158 `hostMode === 'network'\|'custom'\|'local'` decides the listener host; choice: code (the closed set of values the terminal settings file defines). L163 `localhostTarget` maps `0.0.0.0` and `::` to `127.0.0.1`; choice: code (those are the wildcard bind addresses by definition). L166-178 `hostnameFromUrl` and `csv` parse URLs and comma lists; choice: code (URL and CSV grammar). L181-187 `uniqueHosts` ignored set `''`, `0.0.0.0`, `::`, `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` decides which hosts need no allowedHosts entry; choice: code (literal loopback and wildcard addresses). L201-215 `??` chains decide which binding source and env override wins; choice: code (the owner's precedence order). L273-305 `manualChunks` decides a module's chunk by package-name substrings of its `node_modules` path; choice: code (the question is which package the file path names, a path-structure fact, and the table is the owner's bundling assignment). L270 `chunkSizeWarningLimit: 800` decides when the build warns; choice: code (a limit the program sets). |

L149 `request.mode === 'navigate'` decides whether a request is a page navigation; choice: code (a request mode the browser sets). | File | Disposition | Note |

| `src/views/ChatView.test.tsx` | PORT | Target delta (dadf577): Current behavior cases include a terminal turnState from a stream error in session A does not bleed into session B; the switch to the send-created session does not wipe the submitted state. Removed copy/constant/schema-size/source-text assertions are not restored. Helpers and explicit fixture/DOM/HTTP state checks remain code; semantic helper obligations are recorded in their own rows. Baseline rationale (9856; historical lines/removed assertions are not current targets): Tests that a terminal turn state from a stream error in one session does not carry into another after a prop-driven session switch, and that a send which creates its session keeps its in-flight turn state. Checks: L138-145 `waitFor` decides when to stop polling; now a predicate and a 1000 ms timeout; choice: code (a test timeout). L123-129 `badgeText`/`composerErrorText` read DOM text; no decision. The sdk mock returns fixed values and makes no decision. Expectations are unchanged: ChatView.tsx keeps every check as code. |
| `src/views/ChatView.tsx` | PORT | Target delta (dadf577): Adds the new-chat greeting/suggestion state and ChatHeader/composer composition. Draft/session presence, exact turn state, cancellation flags and explicit callbacks remain code; suggestions only populate input. Preserve stream/session switching, title edits, auth errors and tool-result ownership. Baseline rationale (9856; historical lines/removed assertions are not current targets): Composes the chat sub-view (SessionHeader, ChatSearch, MessageList, QueuedMessagesPanel, Composer) over `useChatStream` and `useChatSend`, with provider and model selection, effort selection, rename, search jump, wake-word transcript sink and a client-side auto-title. Checks: L107-115, L124, L129 decide provider merge, the selected provider and whether the current model belongs to it; now id equality and `sortProvidersConfiguredFirst`; choice: code (id equality and a sort on the daemon's `configured` boolean). L118-123, L136-138 decide which field holds the registry key, provider and effort; now `firstString` over named wire keys; choice: code (reads fields of the daemon's JSON by name). L144, L153 decide the fallback title and whether the title is just the session id; now equality with the session id; choice: code. L148 decides the preferred provider; now the daemon's current provider, else the first; choice: code. L181 `effort === ''` decides whether to clear the effort; choice: code (the empty option is the program's own "default"). L199 `retry` decides whether a failed message fetch retries; now `isSessionNotFoundError` and `failureCount < 2`; choice: code (HTTP status predicate and a retry cap the program sets). L205, L362, L540, L561 decide polling, completion resolution and the visible turn badge; now membership of `turnState` in `ACTIVE_TURN_STATES` and exact state names; choice: code (a closed turn-state union the program itself produces). L225 clamps the composer height between 44 and 140 px; choice: code (a size limit). L245 decides whether a session switch is the one the send just created; now id equality; choice: code. L275 `result.cancelled` decides the tool-cancel error text; choice: code (daemon boolean). L310-313 decides whether to fold live tool activity into a message; now id lookup; choice: code. L339-346 decides when the search target can be scrolled to; now `isScrollTargetReady` and `findMessageElement` (search-jump.ts, their own row) by session and message id; choice: code (id equality). L352-358 clears the highlight after 2000 ms; choice: code (a timer). L364, L376-378, L397-398 decide whether an assistant reply exists after the pending user message; now `messageTone(message) === 'assistant'` and `createdAt` comparison; choice: code (reads the role field the daemon writes and compares timestamps). L394-396, L404-411 decide whether the auto-title may overwrite the current title; now whether the session was already auto-titled or hand-renamed, and exact equality of the current title with the strings the program itself writes (`''`, the session id, `New Chat`, the create-time 72-character slice, the derived title); choice: code (equality with titles this program produced; a hand-typed title is tracked separately in `manuallyTitledSessionsRef`). L401-402 the title text itself comes from `deriveChatTitle` (src/views/chat/message-utils.ts), whose choice is recorded in that file's row. L423-428 decide whether a wake transcript is sent or appended; now the `autoSubmit` setting and whether the draft is empty; choice: code (an owner setting and a length test). L447, L474 decide whether a send or copy has anything to act on; choice: code (empty tests). L461, L466, L528, L534 decide steer, submit, cancel and confirm keys; now `shouldSteerComposerKey`/`shouldSubmitComposerKey` and `Escape`/`Enter`; choice: code (keyboard shortcut mapping). L497 `distanceFromBottom > 180` decides whether the jump-to-bottom button shows; choice: code (a pixel distance the program sets). ## Remaining semantic decision points

These source decisions remain required. The former description-length and planning-identifier editorial gates are intentionally retired, not claimed as implemented readings. Contract-tree and gate-preset consumers are additionally listed as JEV in their source/test rows.

| Where | Guessed today | Replaced by |
|---|---|---|
| `vite.config.ts:124` (`daemonSupportsWebuiCommand`) | Whether the installed `goodvibes-daemon` binary offers a `webui` subcommand, guessed by `/\bwebui\b/` matching the word anywhere in its free-text `--help` output (a description line mentioning the webui also matches) | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.devserver.cli-subcommand`: "Does this `--help` listing offer `webui` as a subcommand this binary routes to?" answer yes or no; band set high because a false yes runs `webui status --json` on a build that boots a second daemon for unrecognized arguments |
| `e2e/support/mock-daemon.ts:1865` (`/api/memory/review-queue` handler filter) | Which memory records a person should review: the mock keeps records with confidence below 60 or review state 'fresh', a cutoff plus one state standing in for "needs a person's check" | Jev yes/no per record, battery engine.state.memory-review-priority (item needs_review: should a person check this record before agents keep relying on it); the mock serves the queue ordered by a stubbed needs_review probability per seeded record, matching the goodvibes-jev daemon's reviewQueue, with no confidence cutoff |
| `src/components/CredentialStatusPanel.tsx:126-127` (`matched`) | Whether a credential key belongs to the selected provider: a case-insensitive substring test of the provider id inside the key name (GEMINI_API_KEY misses `google`; AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY matches `openai`) | Jev entity alignment yes/no, proposed battery `webui.credentials.provider-key`: for each credential key K and the selected provider P, "is K a credential provider P uses?", answer yes or no; all keys asked in one fan-out request when a provider is selected; low stakes (a row highlight), so a plain band; the question and band live in the battery file |
| `src/lib/card-material.ts:93` (`isCardMaterialKey`, `CARD_MATERIAL_COMPOUND_SEGMENTS`) | Whether an undeclared config key names a card number, guessed by stripping separators and matching `cardnumber`/`cardnum`/`cardno` | Jev yes/no, proposed battery `webui.settings.card-material-key`: "Does this config key hold payment card material (a card number, security code, expiry or cardholder name) rather than a setting about cards?"; yes drops the key from every settings row source; high stakes band |
| `src/lib/card-material.ts:95` (`isCardMaterialKey`, `CARD_MATERIAL_WORD_TOKENS`) | Whether the key's trailing word names card material, guessed by camelCase splitting and a cvv/cvv2/cvc/cvc2/pan word list | Same reading as L93, one question per key; the exact keys the card-entry surface writes (L90) stay code |
| `src/lib/commands.ts:111` (`scoreCommand`) | Which palette commands best match what the user typed, guessed by a hand-tuned 0-6 tier ladder of prefix and substring positions | Jev rerank, proposed battery `webui.palette.command-rank`: "Which of these commands is the user asking for with this text?" over the registered commands (title, group, keywords), best first; literal presence of the typed text stays code |

| `src/lib/config-redaction.test.ts:112` (`BROAD_CONTENT_SIGNAL`) | Which real schema keys and daemon paths the coverage test treats as secret-shaped, guessed by a keyword regex anywhere in the key | The coverage test asks `webui.settings.secret-config-key` for every schema key and non-schema path and compares the yes answers with SECRET_CONFIG_KEYS in code |

| `src/lib/errors.ts:95` (`isSessionClosedError`) | Whether an error without a code means the session is closed, guessed by `session is closed`/`session closed` substrings | Same battery, item `session_closed`: "Does this error say the session is already closed?" |
| `src/lib/errors.ts:116` (`isSessionActiveError`) | Whether an error without a code means the session must be closed before delete, guessed by `session is active` | Same battery, item `session_active`: "Does this error say the session is still active and has to be closed before it can be deleted?" |

| `src/lib/presentation-bridge.ts:73-99` (`classifyBadgeTone`) | Lower-cases any status string and tests three substring keyword ladders in order (`error`/`fail`/`denied`/`expired` bad, `warn`/`pending`/`blocked`/`expiring` warning, `healthy`/`ok`/`ready`/`active` ok, else neutral), so `token` reads ok, `inactive` reads ok and an unforeseen status reads by its letters | Jev choice `webui.status.badge-tone`: "Which badge tone fits this status as a person would read it?" over {ok, warning, bad, neutral}, one reading per distinct status string; StatusBadge keeps its classes and glyphs |
